### **20.1 Design conclusion**

STEP-189 establishes a proposed distinction between four different questions:

1. Integrity: Is the evidence consistent with its declared content commitments?  
2. Authority: Was the relevant operation authorized under the applicable historical rules?  
3. Interpretation: What does the evidence support, and under which semantic contract?  
4. Reproducibility: Can another verifier reproduce the declared result from the identified inputs?

A system that answers only the first question cannot claim to have answered all four.

STEP-189 status: draft complete. Adoption, implementation, validation, acceptance, and Freeze remain unestablished.

## **21\. NEXT STEP**

### **STEP-190 — Canonical History Proof Protocol / Checkpoint Finality / Verification Composition / Audit Decision Contract**

STEP-190 should connect the governance and verification contracts into an explicit protocol for deciding when a verification result is sufficient for a downstream action.

Planned scope:

1. Formal proof-object model and typed verification claims.  
2. Checkpoint issuance, authorization, finality, and succession.  
3. Range-proof composition and verified-prefix rules.  
4. Proof invalidation and historical reassessment.  
5. Verification-profile compatibility and version pinning.  
6. Composition of integrity, authority, continuity, and semantic claims.  
7. Verification decision versus downstream authorization.  
8. Failure propagation and bounded impact analysis.  
9. Audit decision records and exception governance.  
10. Acceptance evidence for end-to-end proof composition.

Governing constraints:

PROOF\_OBJECT \!= CANONICAL EVENT  
CHECKPOINT \!= COMPLETE HISTORY  
VALID SUBPROOF \!= VALID COMPOSITE PROOF  
VERIFICATION RESULT \!= AUTHORIZATION  
AUTHORIZATION \!= COMMIT  
PROOF INVALIDATION \!= HISTORY REWRITE  
EXCEPTION \!= REQUIREMENT SATISFACTION

STEP-190 must retain the current status distinctions: design proposal, implementation, testing, adoption, canonical mutation, acceptance, and Freeze are independent states. No upstream draft may be treated as an adopted dependency without explicit evidence.

# **STEP-190 — Canonical History Proof Protocol / Checkpoint Finality / Verification Composition / Audit Decision Contract**

STEP\_ID: 190  
VERSION: 0.1.0-draft  
STATUS: DRAFT\_COMPLETE  
NORMATIVE\_STATUS: PROPOSED  
ADOPTION\_STATUS: NOT\_ADOPTED  
IMPLEMENTATION\_STATUS: NOT\_VERIFIED  
TEST\_STATUS: NOT\_EXECUTED  
REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION: NOT\_PERFORMED  
PROOF\_OBJECT\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
CHECKPOINT\_FINALITY\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
RANGE\_PROOF\_COMPOSITION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
VERIFICATION\_COMPOSITION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
PROOF\_INVALIDATION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
AUDIT\_DECISION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
CANONICAL\_MUTATION: NONE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE: NOT\_ASSESSED  
FREEZE\_STATUS: NOT\_AUTHORIZED  
TIMEZONE: Asia/Tokyo

## **1\. PURPOSE**

STEP-190 proposes the protocol that composes individual verification results into a bounded, evidence-backed decision about whether a specified downstream operation may proceed.

The central requirement is to prevent a valid proof of one property from being misinterpreted as proof of unrelated properties.

CANONICAL HISTORY  
    \-\> TYPED CLAIMS  
    \-\> INDIVIDUAL PROOFS  
    \-\> PROOF VALIDATION  
    \-\> COMPOSITION  
    \-\> POLICY EVALUATION  
    \-\> AUDIT DECISION  
    \-\> SEPARATE AUTHORIZATION  
    \-\> SEPARATE CANONICAL COMMIT, IF APPLICABLE

This is a proposed logical flow. It does not assert that the underlying mechanisms exist or that the sequence is implemented.

### **1.1 Architectural axioms**

CANONICAL\_HISTORY\_IS\_AUTHORITATIVE  
PROOF\_OBJECT\_IS\_EVIDENCE  
PROOF\_VALIDITY\_IS\_SCOPE\_BOUND  
SUBPROOF\_VALIDITY\_DOES\_NOT\_IMPLY\_COMPOSITE\_VALIDITY  
VERIFICATION\_IS\_NOT\_AUTHORIZATION  
AUTHORIZATION\_IS\_NOT\_COMMIT  
CHECKPOINT\_FINALITY\_IS\_NOT\_HISTORY\_FINALITY  
PROOF\_INVALIDATION\_DOES\_NOT\_REWRITE\_HISTORY  
EXCEPTION\_IS\_NOT\_REQUIREMENT\_SATISFACTION  
UNKNOWN\_IS\_NOT\_PASS

### **1.2 Scope**

* Typed proof objects and claim identity.  
* Proof validity, scope, and provenance.  
* Checkpoint issuance, authorization, finality, and succession.  
* Range-proof composition and continuity.  
* Composition of integrity, authenticity, authority, policy, and semantic claims.  
* Proof invalidation and reassessment.  
* Profile compatibility and version pinning.  
* Audit decisions and downstream authorization boundaries.  
* Failure propagation and bounded impact.  
* Exceptions, acceptance evidence, and decision reproducibility.

### **1.3 Non-goals**

* Choosing a production proof system or cryptographic algorithm.  
* Declaring any existing checkpoint or verification report valid.  
* Implementing a commit protocol or modifying Canonical History.  
* Automatically resolving conflicting proofs.  
* Allowing an audit decision to bypass Commit Authority.  
* Establishing production acceptance or Freeze.

## **2\. NORMATIVE LANGUAGE AND AUTHORITY BOUNDARY**

`MUST`, `MUST NOT`, `SHOULD`, `SHOULD NOT`, and `MAY` are proposed normative keywords. They acquire binding force only after formal adoption.

| Entity | Permitted role | Boundary |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Proof producer | Construct a proof and identify its claims | Cannot certify its own proof merely by producing it |
| Proof verifier | Evaluate proof validity under a declared profile | Cannot expand the proof's scope |
| Composer | Combine verified claims under explicit rules | Cannot silently omit required claims |
| Policy evaluator | Determine whether requirements for a specified action are satisfied | Cannot establish canonical commit finality |
| Audit decision authority | Issue a bounded decision under its mandate | Cannot rewrite historical evidence |
| Commit Authority | Authorize and perform canonical mutation under the applicable contract | Cannot infer authorization solely from an audit result |
| Replay engine | Derive projections from eligible History | Cannot mutate Canonical History |

A component MAY perform multiple roles only if its responsibilities, inputs, outputs, and authority boundaries remain independently identifiable and testable.

## **3\. TYPED PROOF OBJECT MODEL**

### **3.1 Proof object**

A proof object MUST identify the claim it supports, the subject to which it applies, the verification profile, and the evidence needed to evaluate it.

Proposed schema:

proof\_id: REQUIRED  
proof\_type: REQUIRED  
subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
claim\_id: REQUIRED  
claim\_scope: REQUIRED  
history\_id: REQUIRED  
history\_range: OPTIONAL  
profile\_id: REQUIRED  
profile\_version: REQUIRED  
producer\_identity: REQUIRED  
evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
dependency\_proof\_refs: REQUIRED  
result: REQUIRED  
limitations: REQUIRED

The schema is conceptual and has not been validated. Exact field types, cardinality, canonical serialization, and cryptographic bindings remain open design decisions.

### **3.2 Proof types**

The proof system SHOULD distinguish at least:

CONTENT\_INTEGRITY\_PROOF  
EVENT\_AUTHENTICITY\_PROOF  
AUTHORIZATION\_PROOF  
CONTINUITY\_PROOF  
CHECKPOINT\_PROOF  
RANGE\_COMPLETENESS\_PROOF  
POLICY\_COMPLIANCE\_PROOF  
SEMANTIC\_EVIDENCE\_PROOF  
REPLAY\_EQUIVALENCE\_PROOF  
AUDIT\_EXECUTION\_PROOF

The type declares the intended claim class. It does not establish validity.

An object labelled `AUTHORIZATION_PROOF` MUST NOT be accepted as such unless its contents and verification procedure satisfy the authorization requirements of the adopted profile.

### **3.3 Claim identity**

Claim identity MUST be stable enough to distinguish materially different assertions about the same subject.

At minimum, claim identity MUST account for:

* subject identity;  
* property being asserted;  
* relevant History or range;  
* applicable profile;  
* relevant temporal or authority context, where material.

Two proofs about the same event but different properties MUST NOT be treated as interchangeable.

### **3.4 Proof result**

The proposed proof result vocabulary is:

VALID  
INVALID  
INDETERMINATE  
PARTIALLY\_VALID  
NOT\_EVALUATED

* `VALID`: the proof satisfies the declared verification procedure for its entire declared scope.  
* `INVALID`: a required assertion is disproved or a mandatory proof condition fails.  
* `INDETERMINATE`: evidence is insufficient to determine validity.  
* `PARTIALLY_VALID`: only an explicitly identified subset is verified.  
* `NOT_EVALUATED`: no verification result is available.

A proof producer's claimed result MUST NOT be treated as the verifier's result without the required verification execution.

### **3.5 Proof identity versus event identity**

A proof object and the event it concerns MUST have distinct identities.

Multiple proofs MAY concern the same event. A proof MAY be superseded, invalidated, or retained for historical audit without changing the identity or contents of the original event.

A proof object's existence MUST NOT create a canonical event or modify a committed event.

## **4\. PROOF VALIDITY, SCOPE, AND PROVENANCE**

### **4.1 Scope containment**

Every proof MUST declare the scope over which its conclusion holds.

A proof MUST NOT be applied to:

* a different History;  
* an unproven event range;  
* a different claim type;  
* an incompatible verification profile;  
* an authority epoch or temporal context not covered by its evidence.

Any permitted scope extension MUST be supported by an explicit composition rule and verified evidence.

### **4.2 Scope containment rule**

Let ${S}_{p}$ be the scope established by proof $p$, and ${S}_{c}$ the scope required by a downstream claim.

A proof may directly support that claim only if:

${S}_{c}\subseteq {S}_{p}$

and the claim type, profile, dependencies, and relevant context are compatible.

This relation is necessary but not sufficient. Scope containment alone does not prove that the proof is valid.

### **4.3 Provenance**

Proof provenance MUST identify the producer, inputs, method or profile, and relevant execution evidence.

Where a proof depends on another proof, that dependency MUST reference the exact proof identity and version—not merely a mutable alias or descriptive label.

### **4.4 Self-asserted proof status**

A proof object MUST NOT become valid because it contains `result: VALID`.

The verifier MUST derive its result from the declared proof contents, evidence, profile, and verification procedure.

### **4.5 Proof content immutability**

Once a proof object has been issued and referenced by an audit decision, its content MUST NOT be silently changed.

A changed proof MUST receive a distinct identity or version, with an explicit relationship to the earlier object. Any affected downstream decision MUST be reevaluated according to the applicable policy.

## **5\. CHECKPOINT ISSUANCE, AUTHORIZATION, AND FINALITY**

### **5.1 Checkpoint lifecycle**

The proposed checkpoint lifecycle is:

CHECKPOINT\_CANDIDATE  
  \-\> CHECKPOINT\_VALIDATED  
  \-\> CHECKPOINT\_AUTHORIZED  
  \-\> CHECKPOINT\_ISSUED  
  \-\> CHECKPOINT\_VERIFIED  
  \-\> CHECKPOINT\_SUPERSEDED \| CHECKPOINT\_INVALIDATED

These are distinct states. A checkpoint may be issued but not yet verified by a particular verifier. Supersession does not necessarily mean invalidity.

### **5.2 Checkpoint identity and binding**

A checkpoint MUST be bound to:

* the logical `history_id`;  
* the exact History prefix or range;  
* the declared terminal event or sequence;  
* the commitment or proof construction used;  
* the governing checkpoint profile;  
* the issuing authority and applicable authority context;  
* the evidence needed to verify issuance and content.

### **5.3 Issuance versus verification**

Checkpoint issuance is an act by a designated authority. Checkpoint verification is an assessment of the resulting evidence.

Neither may be substituted for the other.

ISSUED \!= VERIFIED  
VERIFIED \!= AUTHORIZED  
AUTHORIZED \!= COMMITTED

A checkpoint MAY be used as a trust anchor for a later range only when the adopted profile explicitly permits it and the required checkpoint authorization and verification claims pass.

### **5.4 Checkpoint finality**

The contract MUST define the conditions under which an issued checkpoint is considered authoritative for its declared prefix.

Those conditions MUST address conflicting issuance, authority epochs, proof integrity, and the applicable commit or checkpoint protocol.

A checkpoint MUST NOT be declared final merely because it has been signed, replicated, timestamped, or observed by multiple nodes.

The precise checkpoint-finality protocol remains unresolved until the upstream Commit Finality and authority-transfer contracts are adopted and reconciled.

### **5.5 Checkpoint succession**

A successor checkpoint MUST identify its predecessor or otherwise prove the required continuity relationship.

If the successor covers a larger range, the proof MUST establish that the new range extends the previously verified prefix under the adopted protocol.

A successor checkpoint MUST NOT silently replace a conflicting checkpoint. Conflicts require preservation of both records and an explicit adjudication path.

### **5.6 Checkpoint invalidation**

A checkpoint MAY become unusable for a particular claim because its proof fails, its required trust assumptions are no longer satisfied, or later evidence establishes a conflict.

Invalidation MUST identify the reason, affected scope, evidence, and applicable policy.

Invalidating a checkpoint does not delete the underlying History and does not automatically establish that every event in the covered range was invalid.

## **6\. RANGE-PROOF COMPOSITION AND VERIFIED-PREFIX RULES**

### **6.1 Composition objective**

Range-proof composition MUST establish that individually verified segments form a valid proof for the requested aggregate range.

The validity of every individual segment is necessary where every segment is required, but it is not sufficient. Composition MUST additionally establish compatible History identity, boundary alignment, ordering, continuity, and completeness.

P0: GENESIS \-\> CHECKPOINT A  
P1: CHECKPOINT A \-\> CHECKPOINT B  
P2: CHECKPOINT B \-\> CHECKPOINT C  
                         \|  
                         v  
              COMPOSITE RANGE PROOF  
                         \|  
                         v  
        IDENTITY \+ BOUNDARY \+ CONTINUITY  
              \+ COMPLETENESS CHECKS

This diagram represents a possible composition pattern, not a selected production proof construction.

### **6.2 Composition preconditions**

A composite proof MUST establish, as required by its profile:

1. Every required subproof has a distinct, resolvable identity.  
2. Every required subproof is verified under a compatible profile.  
3. All subproofs refer to the intended logical History.  
4. Adjacent range boundaries match or have an explicitly verified transition.  
5. Ordering and sequence semantics are compatible.  
6. No required interval is omitted.  
7. No conflicting or duplicate interval is silently accepted.  
8. The composition rule itself is valid for the declared proof types.  
9. Every dependency needed to establish completeness is resolved.  
10. The composite proof identifies its complete input set.

### **6.3 Boundary semantics**

The protocol MUST define whether range boundaries are inclusive or exclusive and how event sequence numbers, predecessor references, and terminal checkpoints are interpreted.

Two adjacent ranges MUST NOT be considered continuous merely because their displayed numeric boundaries appear adjacent. Their actual boundary references and the adopted ordering model must establish the relationship.

### **6.4 Gap and overlap handling**

A range composition MUST explicitly detect:

* missing intervals;  
* overlapping intervals;  
* conflicting event identities;  
* duplicate sequence positions;  
* incompatible predecessor references;  
* conflicting terminal checkpoints;  
* unsupported branch transitions.

An overlap MAY be accepted when the composition profile explicitly permits it and verifies that the overlapping content is consistent. An overlap MUST NOT be silently discarded to make the range appear continuous.

A gap MUST remain visible unless an adopted proof construction establishes that the apparent gap is valid under the relevant History model.

### **6.5 Composite result**

The composite proof MUST retain references to all required subproofs and record the result of every mandatory composition condition.

The composite result MUST NOT be stronger than its verified dependencies and composition checks.

COMPOSITE\_VALID  
    requires  
      ALL\_REQUIRED\_SUBPROOFS\_VALID  
      AND IDENTITY\_COMPATIBILITY  
      AND BOUNDARY\_COMPATIBILITY  
      AND CONTINUITY\_ESTABLISHED  
      AND REQUIRED\_COMPLETENESS\_ESTABLISHED  
      AND COMPOSITION\_RULE\_VALID

If a required subproof is indeterminate, the composite MUST NOT be `VALID` unless the adopted profile explicitly establishes that the subproof is unnecessary for the specific claim.

### **6.6 Verified-prefix semantics**

A verified prefix is a contiguous range whose required claims have been established from the declared genesis or an accepted, verified checkpoint.

The system MUST record the exact endpoint of that prefix. A verified prefix MUST NOT imply that the remainder of the History is verified.

If verification fails at event ${e}_{k}$, a preceding range MAY remain verified if its proof dependencies are independent of the failed range. The system MUST determine the affected scope from the actual dependency graph rather than assuming either total invalidity or total independence.

### **6.7 Composite proof identity**

Every composite proof MUST have its own identity and identify:

* the requested aggregate scope;  
* the ordered subproof references;  
* the composition algorithm or rule-set version;  
* the compatibility decisions;  
* the resulting verified and unverified ranges;  
* the complete verification result.

Changing any material input or composition rule MUST produce a new composite proof or a new version under an explicitly defined immutable-version model.

## **7\. VERIFICATION COMPOSITION ACROSS CLAIM TYPES**

### **7.1 Claim dimensions**

The protocol MUST represent distinct claims for distinct properties.

| Claim dimension | Required question |
| ----- | ----- |
| Content integrity | Does the content satisfy its declared integrity commitment? |
| Authenticity | Does the evidence establish the relevant cryptographic identity relationship? |
| Authorization | Was the action authorized under the applicable authority and policy? |
| Continuity | Is the event connected to the declared History range? |
| Completeness | Is the required range present under the selected proof construction? |
| Policy compliance | Were the applicable normative constraints satisfied? |
| Semantic support | Does the evidence support the asserted interpretation? |
| Replay equivalence | Does replay produce the expected derived result under the declared profile? |

These dimensions MUST NOT be collapsed into one undifferentiated proof type.

### **7.2 Required-claim manifest**

Every composite verification request MUST identify the set of claims required for its intended decision.

Proposed manifest:

verification\_request\_id: REQUIRED  
subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
requested\_scope: REQUIRED  
decision\_purpose: REQUIRED  
verification\_profile\_id: REQUIRED  
required\_claims: REQUIRED  
optional\_claims: REQUIRED  
permitted\_alternatives: REQUIRED  
policy\_ref: REQUIRED

A verifier MUST NOT silently remove a difficult claim from the required set. A permitted alternative MUST be explicitly declared by the governing profile and independently verified.

### **7.3 Claim dependency graph**

The required claim set SHOULD be represented as a dependency graph. A claim MUST be considered established only when its own proof requirements and all mandatory dependencies are satisfied.

For example, a downstream authorization decision might require:

CONTENT INTEGRITY  
        \+  
EVENT AUTHENTICITY  
        \+  
HISTORY CONTINUITY  
        \+  
COMMIT AUTHORIZATION  
        \+  
APPLICABLE POLICY  
        \|  
        v  
ELIGIBILITY CLAIM  
        \|  
        v  
SEPARATE AUTHORIZATION DECISION

The graph is illustrative. The actual dependencies depend on the downstream operation and its adopted policy.

### **7.4 No implicit claim promotion**

The system MUST NOT infer:

* authorization from integrity;  
* continuity from a valid signature;  
* completeness from a valid terminal checkpoint alone;  
* semantic truth from replay equivalence;  
* commit finality from an authorization proof;  
* current trust from historical trust evidence without evaluating the relevant trust policy.

Any such inference requires an explicit normative rule and supporting evidence.

### **7.5 Contradictory claims**

If two verified-looking proofs make incompatible claims about the same subject, scope, and relevant context, the composer MUST detect the conflict where the declared claim model permits that comparison.

The composer MUST NOT resolve the conflict using arbitrary priority, arrival order, producer reputation alone, or whichever result is more favorable.

The conflicting proofs MUST be retained, and the aggregate claim MUST remain unresolved until an applicable adjudication rule determines the result.

### **7.6 Semantic evidence boundary**

A semantic-support proof establishes only the proposition defined by its claim and verification profile.

If an LLM produces a proposed interpretation, the proposal MUST NOT be treated as a verified semantic claim merely because it is syntactically valid or passes a schema validator.

The system MUST identify the evidence, deterministic checks, review requirements, and acceptance criteria needed to establish the relevant semantic claim.

## **8\. PROOF INVALIDATION AND HISTORICAL REASSESSMENT**

### **8.1 Invalidation triggers**

A proof MAY require invalidation or reassessment when:

* its content commitment fails verification;  
* required evidence is found to be forged or misattributed;  
* its authority context is invalidated;  
* a conflicting checkpoint or History fork is discovered;  
* a previously unknown dependency becomes material;  
* a verification algorithm or trust assumption is found inadequate;  
* the profile under which the proof was produced is withdrawn or superseded;  
* new evidence materially changes the supported claim.

These triggers do not all have identical effects. The adopted policy MUST define whether each trigger causes invalidation, restricted applicability, or reassessment.

### **8.2 Proof status versus historical event status**

Proof invalidation and event invalidation MUST remain distinct.

PROOF\_INVALIDATED  
    \!= EVENT\_ERASED  
    \!= EVENT\_NEVER\_COMMITTED  
    \!= EVENT\_AUTOMATICALLY\_INVALID

A proof may cease to establish a claim even though the underlying event remains part of Canonical History.

Whether the event was validly committed MUST be evaluated under the applicable commit and governance contracts.

### **8.3 Invalidation record**

An invalidation record SHOULD contain:

invalidation\_id: REQUIRED  
target\_proof\_ref: REQUIRED  
trigger\_type: REQUIRED  
affected\_claim\_refs: REQUIRED  
affected\_scope: REQUIRED  
evidence\_refs: REQUIRED  
decision\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
governing\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
effective\_scope: REQUIRED  
reassessment\_requirements: REQUIRED

The invalidation record MUST identify whether the decision concerns a specific proof, an entire proof family, a verification profile, or a broader set of claims.

### **8.4 Dependency propagation**

When a proof is invalidated, dependent composite proofs and audit decisions MUST be identified through the recorded dependency graph.

The system MUST NOT assume that every downstream result is automatically invalid. It MUST evaluate whether the invalidated proof was a required dependency for each affected claim.

If the dependency graph is incomplete, the affected downstream scope MUST be marked unresolved rather than assumed unaffected.

### **8.5 Historical report preservation**

Previously issued reports MUST remain identifiable after reassessment.

A new report MUST reference the prior report and state whether it:

* confirms the previous result;  
* narrows its verified scope;  
* invalidates a previous claim;  
* adds a new limitation;  
* supersedes the earlier assessment under a new profile.

The system MUST NOT silently rewrite an old report to match the latest conclusion.

### **8.6 No automatic canonical mutation**

Proof invalidation MUST NOT itself modify Canonical History.

If a resulting finding requires a correction or compensating event, that operation MUST use the separately adopted History correction and Commit Authority protocols.

## **9\. VERIFICATION-PROFILE COMPATIBILITY AND VERSION PINNING**

### **9.1 Profile identity**

Every proof and composite decision MUST identify the exact verification profile and version under which its result was established.

A mutable profile alias such as `latest` MUST NOT be the only reference in an audit-critical record.

### **9.2 Compatibility determination**

Two proofs are compatible only if their declared profiles permit composition for the intended claim.

Compatibility MUST account for, where material:

* History identity and ordering model;  
* proof type and claim semantics;  
* schema versions;  
* cryptographic algorithms and parameters;  
* trust policies and authority epochs;  
* boundary and completeness semantics;  
* normalization and canonicalization rules;  
* relevant validator and composition versions.

Matching version numbers do not, by themselves, prove compatibility.

### **9.3 Profile evolution**

A new profile version MUST identify the requirements it changes and the implications for existing proofs.

A profile update MUST NOT silently change the meaning of a proof already issued under an earlier profile.

Historical proofs MAY remain valid under their original profile while being insufficient for a new decision requiring stronger assurance. The system MUST report these as separate claims.

### **9.4 Reverification**

When a new profile requires re-verification, the system MUST retain the original result and record the new verification execution separately.

The new result MUST identify its inputs, profile, verifier, scope, and differences from the prior assessment.

### **9.5 Unsupported profiles**

If a required profile or dependency cannot be loaded or verified, the system MUST NOT silently downgrade to a weaker profile.

The decision MUST remain blocked or unresolved unless the governing policy explicitly permits an alternative profile and that alternative is independently identified and evaluated.

## **10\. AUDIT DECISION CONTRACT**

### **10.1 Decision purpose**

An audit decision evaluates whether specified evidence satisfies the requirements for a named downstream action.

It does not itself grant unrestricted authority, create a canonical event, or establish facts beyond the verified claims.

### **10.2 Decision states**

The proposed decision vocabulary is:

ALLOW  
DENY  
HOLD  
ALLOW\_WITH\_EXCEPTION  
NOT\_EVALUATED

* `ALLOW`: all mandatory requirements for the specified decision are satisfied.  
* `DENY`: an applicable policy or verified finding prohibits the requested action.  
* `HOLD`: evidence, dependency, conflict, or authority status remains unresolved.  
* `ALLOW_WITH_EXCEPTION`: a formally permitted exception has been authorized and recorded.  
* `NOT_EVALUATED`: no completed decision evaluation exists.

`ALLOW_WITH_EXCEPTION` MUST NOT be represented as ordinary full compliance. Its scope, authorization, expiry or review condition, and residual risk MUST be recorded.

### **10.3 Decision record**

Proposed schema:

audit\_decision\_id: REQUIRED  
decision\_subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
decision\_purpose: REQUIRED  
requested\_action: REQUIRED  
requested\_scope: REQUIRED  
required\_claim\_manifest\_ref: REQUIRED  
composite\_proof\_refs: REQUIRED  
policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
decision: REQUIRED  
decision\_reason\_codes: REQUIRED  
unresolved\_claim\_refs: REQUIRED  
exception\_ref: OPTIONAL  
decision\_authority\_ref: REQUIRED  
decision\_time\_evidence\_ref: OPTIONAL  
decision\_evidence\_ref: REQUIRED

The exact schema remains unvalidated. A decision MUST identify the action and scope it addresses; a general-purpose `ALLOW` without a declared target MUST NOT be accepted.

### **10.4 Decision derivation**

The decision evaluator MUST apply a versioned, deterministic rule set to the required claims and applicable policy.

The evaluator MUST NOT infer missing evidence, repair invalid proofs, or silently reinterpret the requested action.

The evaluator MUST preserve the difference between a policy violation and an inability to establish compliance.

### **10.5 Decision versus authorization**

An audit decision MAY be a prerequisite for authorization, but MUST NOT automatically be treated as authorization unless the adopted governance contract explicitly assigns that authority to the decision mechanism.

Where Commit Authority is separate, the decision MUST be supplied as evidence to the authorization process rather than bypassing it.

### **10.6 Decision expiry and re-evaluation**

A decision MAY be time-limited or conditional on the continued validity of specified proofs.

Where the policy requires freshness, the decision MUST identify its validity conditions. A material proof invalidation, authority revocation, profile withdrawal, or scope change MUST trigger reevaluation when required by that policy.

A decision MUST NOT be treated as perpetually valid merely because it was once issued.

## **11\. FAILURE PROPAGATION AND BOUNDED IMPACT ANALYSIS**

### **11.1 Failure propagation model**

The system MUST distinguish a failure in an individual proof from a failure in a composite claim, a downstream decision, or Canonical History itself.

PROOF FAILURE  
    \|  
    v  
DEPENDENCY GRAPH LOOKUP  
    \|  
    v  
AFFECTED CLAIM IDENTIFICATION  
    \|  
    v  
COMPOSITE PROOF REASSESSMENT  
    \|  
    v  
AUDIT DECISION REASSESSMENT  
    \|  
    v  
DOWNSTREAM ACTION CONTROL

This flow does not authorize automatic History modification.

### **11.2 Impact classes**

The proposed impact classes are:

| Class | Meaning |
| ----- | ----- |
| `LOCAL_PROOF_IMPACT` | A single proof or claim is affected. |
| `RANGE_IMPACT` | A declared History range or checkpoint dependency is affected. |
| `COMPOSITE_IMPACT` | A composite proof depends on affected evidence. |
| `DECISION_IMPACT` | A prior audit decision may no longer satisfy its requirements. |
| `PROFILE_IMPACT` | A verification profile or common rule affects multiple results. |
| `HISTORY_INTEGRITY_IMPACT` | Evidence indicates a possible canonical continuity or integrity problem. |
| `IMPACT_UNKNOWN` | The dependency graph or available evidence is insufficient to bound the impact. |

These classes are proposed. Their exact transition and escalation rules require adoption.

### **11.3 Minimum affected scope**

Impact analysis SHOULD identify the smallest scope that can be justified by the dependency graph and proof semantics.

The system MUST NOT claim that the impact is limited to a single proof if shared dependencies may affect other proofs and that possibility has not been evaluated.

Likewise, a localized failure MUST NOT be used to declare the entire History invalid without supporting evidence.

### **11.4 Shared dependency failure**

If a shared verifier, trust anchor, schema, cryptographic primitive, or profile is found defective, the system MUST identify all known results that depend on the affected component.

Where dependency tracking is incomplete, the system MUST record the uncertainty and the scope requiring investigation.

### **11.5 Downstream action containment**

If an affected proof was a mandatory prerequisite for a downstream action, the applicable policy MUST determine whether the action is blocked, suspended, reevaluated, or allowed to continue under a documented exception.

A past `ALLOW` decision MUST NOT automatically authorize new actions after a relevant prerequisite has been invalidated.

The system MUST distinguish a historical action already performed from a future action that has not yet occurred. A reassessment MUST NOT retroactively erase an action from history.

### **11.6 Impact report**

An impact report SHOULD contain:

impact\_report\_id: REQUIRED  
trigger\_ref: REQUIRED  
dependency\_graph\_version: REQUIRED  
affected\_proofs: REQUIRED  
affected\_claims: REQUIRED  
affected\_ranges: REQUIRED  
affected\_decisions: REQUIRED  
unresolved\_scope: REQUIRED  
containment\_actions: REQUIRED  
evidence\_refs: REQUIRED

The report MUST distinguish confirmed dependencies from possible dependencies and unexamined scope.

## **12\. EXCEPTION GOVERNANCE**

### **12.1 Exception conditions**

An exception MAY be permitted only when the applicable governance policy explicitly allows it.

An exception MUST NOT be used to silently waive a hard invariant, manufacture a missing proof, or redefine a failed check as successful.

### **12.2 Required exception record**

An exception SHOULD identify:

exception\_id: REQUIRED  
target\_requirement\_refs: REQUIRED  
target\_claim\_refs: REQUIRED  
requested\_scope: REQUIRED  
reason: REQUIRED  
risk\_assessment\_ref: REQUIRED  
compensating\_controls: REQUIRED  
authorizing\_identity: REQUIRED  
governing\_policy\_ref: REQUIRED  
effective\_conditions: REQUIRED  
expiry\_or\_review\_condition: REQUIRED  
evidence\_refs: REQUIRED

The exception schema and approval workflow remain design proposals.

### **12.3 Exception limits**

An exception MUST be limited to the declared subject, scope, purpose, and conditions. It MUST NOT automatically propagate to other histories, ranges, profiles, or decisions.

An exception MUST NOT establish that an unmet technical requirement has been satisfied. It records a separate governance decision permitting a bounded departure from a requirement, where such departure is allowed.

### **12.4 Prohibited exceptions**

Under this proposed contract, an exception MUST NOT:

* fabricate or replace missing historical evidence;  
* silently rewrite Canonical History;  
* bypass a mandatory exclusive-authority requirement;  
* conceal a known conflicting History;  
* falsely label an unverified proof as verified;  
* convert an unresolved commit status into a confirmed commit;  
* suppress a required audit finding.

### **12.5 Exception expiry and revocation**

An exception MUST have an explicit validity condition, expiry, or review mechanism. If its prerequisites cease to hold, the applicable decision MUST be reevaluated.

Exception revocation MUST be recorded as a new governance event or decision. It MUST NOT delete the historical record of the exception.

## **13\. END-TO-END PROOF AND DECISION REPRODUCIBILITY**

### **13.1 Reproduction objective**

An independent evaluator SHOULD be able to reconstruct why a decision was reached from its declared inputs and rules, without relying on hidden mutable state.

The reproducibility target is the decision procedure and its evidence—not merely a summary sentence or a Boolean output.

### **13.2 Required reproducibility inputs**

A reproducible decision record MUST identify, as required by its profile:

* the requested action and scope;  
* the required-claim manifest;  
* all composite and subproof references;  
* the verification profiles and versions;  
* the applicable policy version;  
* the proof-composition rule version;  
* the decision evaluator version;  
* the relevant trust and schema bundles;  
* the recorded inputs and evaluation results;  
* any exception or override decision.

### **13.3 Deterministic evaluation**

The decision evaluator SHOULD be deterministic for a fixed set of canonicalized inputs, profile versions, and policy rules.

If environmental or external state affects the decision, the evaluator MUST identify that state and record sufficient evidence to explain the observed outcome.

A nondeterministic evaluation MUST NOT be presented as deterministic merely because repeated runs happened to produce the same result.

### **13.4 Decision comparison**

When two executions disagree, the comparison MUST identify differences in:

* input proof identities or content;  
* verification outcomes;  
* policy and profile versions;  
* dependency resolution;  
* exception state;  
* evaluation environment;  
* decision rules.

The system MUST NOT select the favorable result without an explicit adjudication rule.

### **13.5 Audit trail**

The system MUST preserve the relationship between the decision and the evidence used to reach it.

Where a later decision supersedes an earlier one, both decisions MUST remain distinguishable, and the superseding decision MUST identify its predecessor and reason.

### **13.6 Reproducibility limits**

Reproducing a decision proves that the declared procedure yields a particular result from the declared inputs, subject to the execution and trust assumptions.

It does not independently prove that the inputs accurately represented external reality or that the decision policy itself was correct.

## **14\. INVARIANTS — STI-190-01..20**

All invariants below are proposed and unverified.

| ID | Invariant | Violation consequence |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| STI-190-01 | Every proof identifies a typed claim, subject, and scope. | Proof rejected |
| STI-190-02 | Proof validity is established by verification, not by self-asserted status. | Result rejected |
| STI-190-03 | Proof identity is distinct from event identity. | Identity conflict |
| STI-190-04 | A proof cannot be applied outside its verified scope without an explicit composition rule. | Scope expansion rejected |
| STI-190-05 | Checkpoint issuance and checkpoint verification remain distinct. | Checkpoint claim unresolved |
| STI-190-06 | Checkpoint finality requires the adopted authority and finality conditions. | Finality not established |
| STI-190-07 | A successor checkpoint cannot silently replace a conflicting checkpoint. | Conflict adjudication required |
| STI-190-08 | Range composition verifies History identity, boundaries, continuity, and required completeness. | Composite proof rejected |
| STI-190-09 | A verified prefix does not imply that the suffix is verified. | Scope claim rejected |
| STI-190-10 | Composite validity requires every mandatory dependency and composition condition. | Composite proof invalid or unresolved |
| STI-190-11 | Integrity, authenticity, authorization, continuity, policy, and semantic claims remain distinct. | Claim composition rejected |
| STI-190-12 | Contradictory claims are not resolved by arrival order or arbitrary preference. | Claim remains unresolved |
| STI-190-13 | Proof invalidation does not itself modify Canonical History. | Operation blocked |
| STI-190-14 | Invalidated dependencies trigger bounded downstream reassessment. | Dependent decisions marked unresolved |
| STI-190-15 | Proofs and decisions identify exact profile versions. | Compatibility unverified |
| STI-190-16 | Unsupported profiles cannot silently downgrade verification requirements. | Decision blocked |
| STI-190-17 | Audit decisions are scoped and distinct from authorization and commit. | Decision cannot authorize by implication |
| STI-190-18 | Exceptions remain explicit, bounded, and separately authorized. | Exception rejected |
| STI-190-19 | Decision reproducibility depends on identified inputs and versioned rules. | Reproducibility claim rejected |
| STI-190-20 | Historical proofs, decisions, and adjudications remain traceable after reassessment. | Audit trail integrity violation |

## **15\. TEST CANDIDATES — ST-190-01..20**

These are proposed test cases. No test implementation or execution is claimed.

| Test ID | Scenario | Expected result |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| ST-190-01 | Submit a proof without a typed claim or explicit scope. | Proof rejected. |
| ST-190-02 | Set a proof's declared result to `VALID` without verification evidence. | Self-asserted status is not accepted. |
| ST-190-03 | Reuse a proof ID for materially different proof contents. | Identity conflict detected. |
| ST-190-04 | Apply a valid proof to a different History. | Scope validation fails. |
| ST-190-05 | Issue a checkpoint without satisfying the required finality conditions. | Finality remains unestablished. |
| ST-190-06 | Verify a checkpoint signature without verifying its range and authority claims. | Only the verified dimensions pass. |
| ST-190-07 | Supply conflicting successor checkpoints. | Both are preserved; adjudication required. |
| ST-190-08 | Compose two ranges with an unexplained gap. | Composite continuity fails. |
| ST-190-09 | Verify a prefix while the requested suffix remains unavailable. | Prefix may pass; suffix remains unverified. |
| ST-190-10 | Compose valid subproofs using an incompatible composition profile. | Composite proof rejected. |
| ST-190-11 | Supply an integrity proof without required authorization evidence. | Integrity may pass; authorization remains unverified. |
| ST-190-12 | Supply contradictory claims about the same event and scope. | Conflict detected; no arbitrary winner. |
| ST-190-13 | Invalidate a proof that does not affect an independent History range. | Only justified dependent scope is reassessed. |
| ST-190-14 | Invalidate a proof required by a prior audit decision. | Dependency and decision impact identified. |
| ST-190-15 | Replace a pinned verification profile with a different version. | Compatibility or re-verification required. |
| ST-190-16 | Request verification using an unsupported profile. | No silent downgrade. |
| ST-190-17 | Treat an audit `ALLOW` as a canonical commit authorization without the required authority. | Authorization boundary blocks the operation. |
| ST-190-18 | Submit an exception without an authorized decision or defined scope. | Exception rejected. |
| ST-190-19 | Repeat decision evaluation with changed policy inputs but unchanged input manifest. | Reproducibility or manifest validation fails. |
| ST-190-20 | Supersede a decision after proof invalidation. | Prior decision remains traceable; new decision records the change. |

### **15.1 Execution requirements**

Every executed test MUST identify the implementation revision, test version, fixtures, applicable verification profile, expected behavior, observed behavior, and supporting evidence.

### **18.1 Artifact constraints**

1. Schemas define structural constraints; dedicated validators MUST enforce cross-record invariants and proof composition.  
2. Proof registries MUST NOT accept a proof type merely because its label is recognized.  
3. Every composite proof MUST preserve references to its exact dependencies.  
4. Every decision MUST identify its required claims, governing policy, and evaluation result.  
5. Exceptions MUST be separate records and MUST NOT overwrite failed requirements.  
6. Test fixtures MUST remain distinguishable from production verification evidence.  
7. Artifact creation or successful schema validation MUST NOT imply formal adoption.

STEP-190 does not assume that STEP-187, STEP-188, or STEP-189 has been adopted.

The following dependencies remain especially significant:

* STEP-187: Commit Finality must be defined before checkpoint finality can rely on canonical commit evidence.  
* STEP-188: Correction, authority transfer, and evidence retention must define how historical changes in assessment are recorded.  
* STEP-189: Verification claims, trust evaluation, and proof provenance must provide the evidence consumed by the composition protocol.

A downstream contract MUST NOT convert an unresolved upstream requirement into an assumed guarantee.

### **20.1 Design conclusion**

STEP-190 proposes a separation of four mechanisms:

1. Proof verification establishes whether a declared proof supports a bounded claim.  
2. Proof composition establishes whether the required claims jointly support a larger claim.  
3. Audit decision evaluates those claims against a declared policy for a specified action.  
4. Authorization and commit remain governed by their own authority and canonical mutation protocols.

This prevents the existence of a valid subproof, a successful audit, or a signed checkpoint from silently acquiring authority it was never granted.

STEP-190 status: draft complete. Adoption, implementation, validation, acceptance, and Freeze remain unestablished.

## **21\. NEXT STEP**

### **STEP-191 — Canonical History Decision Enforcement / Authorization Binding / Commit Admission / Policy Execution Contract**

The next draft should define the boundary between a valid audit decision and the actual authorization and admission of an operation into Canonical History.

Planned scope:

1. Binding an audit decision to a specific action, subject, and scope.  
2. Authorization tokens or decision evidence and their exact semantics.  
3. Decision freshness, expiry, revocation, and replay prevention.  
4. Policy evaluation and deterministic enforcement.  
5. Commit admission preconditions and rejection semantics.  
6. Atomic binding between authorization and the attempted operation.  
7. Time-of-check/time-of-use (TOCTOU) prevention.  
8. Idempotent authorization and commit retry behavior.  
9. Separation of admission, authorization, and commit finality.  
10. Auditable denial, exception, and recovery paths.

Governing constraints:

VALID\_PROOF \!= AUDIT\_DECISION  
AUDIT\_DECISION \!= AUTHORIZATION  
AUTHORIZATION \!= COMMIT\_ADMISSION  
COMMIT\_ADMISSION \!= COMMIT\_FINALITY  
STALE\_DECISION \!= CURRENT\_PERMISSION  
REVOKED\_AUTHORITY \!= VALID\_AUTHORIZATION  
REJECTION \=\> NO\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION

STEP-191 must preserve the existing status discipline: all requirements remain proposals until explicitly adopted, implemented, and verified. No upstream draft may be treated as a production guarantee without evidence of compatibility and acceptance.

## **1\. 目的・適用範囲**

本契約は、STEP-190 で区別した `Proof Verification`、`Audit Decision`、`Authorization`、`Commit Admission`、`Commit Finality` を実行時に混同なく強制するための規範候補を定義する。

対象：

1. 判定と実行要求の厳密な結合。  
2. 認可証拠の意味論と適用範囲。  
3. 判定の鮮度、期限、失効、リプレイ防止。  
4. 決定論的 Policy Evaluation。  
5. Commit Admission の必要条件。  
6. TOCTOU（検査時と使用時の状態乖離）の防止。  
7. Retry、冪等性、結果不明状態の扱い。  
8. 拒否・例外・復旧経路の監査可能性。  
9. Canonical History に対する非変更保証。

### **1.1 非目標**

* Canonical History の再設計。  
* STEP-190 の Proof Object や Audit Decision の再定義。  
* 具体的な暗号方式・トークン形式の最終選定。  
* 既存コードの変更、実装、実行試験。  
* 例外による認可要件の暗黙的な緩和。  
* 未確認の Commit を成功または失敗と推定すること。  
* 本ドラフト単独による Freeze、採用、全体受入れ。

## **2\. 規範原則と権限境界**

以下を本STEPの基礎制約とする。

VALID\_PROOF \!= AUDIT\_DECISION  
AUDIT\_DECISION \!= AUTHORIZATION  
AUTHORIZATION \!= COMMIT\_ADMISSION  
COMMIT\_ADMISSION \!= COMMIT\_FINALITY

STALE\_DECISION \!= CURRENT\_PERMISSION  
REVOKED\_AUTHORITY \!= VALID\_AUTHORIZATION  
REJECTION \=\> NO\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION

| 機構 | 判定対象 | それ自体では保証しないこと |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Proof Verification | 証拠の妥当性 | 操作の許可 |
| Audit Decision | 指定スコープに対する判定 | 現在の認可、Commit |
| Authorization | 特定操作の実行権限 | Commit の受理・永続化 |
| Commit Admission | Commit 処理開始の必要条件 | 永続化の成功 |
| Commit Finality | Commit の確定状態 | それ以外の操作の許可 |

権威境界： Policy と Authorization の検証結果を消費する決定論的 Kernel が、Commit Admission を唯一制御する。LLM、Projection、UI、外部 Agent は、単独で認可・Admission・Commit Finality を確定できない。

本契約が提案する追加の規範制約：

UNBOUND\_DECISION \=\> NO\_ADMISSION  
UNBOUND\_AUTHORIZATION \=\> NO\_ADMISSION  
EXPIRED\_AUTHORIZATION \=\> NO\_ADMISSION  
REVOKED\_AUTHORITY \=\> NO\_ADMISSION  
POLICY\_INDETERMINATE \=\> NO\_ADMISSION  
ADMISSION \!= DURABLE\_COMMIT  
UNKNOWN\_COMMIT\_STATUS \!= COMMIT\_FAILURE

## **3\. 用語と規範的意味**

* Action Request：実行を要求する操作と、その対象・引数・適用スコープを含む要求。  
* Decision Binding：Audit Decision と Action Request の同一性・適用範囲を検証可能な形で結合すること。  
* Authorization Evidence：操作を許可する権限、ポリシー評価、委任などを裏付ける検証可能な証拠。  
* Admission Record：Commit Admission の判定結果と、その判定に用いた入力・バージョンを記録する証跡。  
* Admission Key：操作の同一性と再試行の扱いを識別するためのキー。  
* TOCTOU：認可・ポリシー検査時と Commit 時の間で、対象状態や権限が変化する問題。  
* Commit Finality：Canonical History への変更が、実装で定義された永続化・確定条件を満たした状態。

規範語 `MUST`、`MUST NOT`、`SHOULD`、`MAY` は採用後の要件候補を示す。現在はすべて提案段階であり、既存システムに適用済みであることを意味しない。

## **4\. Action Binding / Decision Identity**

### **4.1 要求の識別**

Action Request は、少なくとも以下を識別可能でなければならない。

action\_request:  
  request\_id: REQUIRED  
  action\_type: REQUIRED  
  subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_ref: REQUIRED  
  scope: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_history\_ref: REQUIRED  
  expected\_history\_position: REQUIRED  
  payload\_digest: REQUIRED  
  requested\_by: REQUIRED  
  idempotency\_key: REQUIRED

各フィールドの具体的な型・正規化・ダイジェスト方式は別途 Schema Contract で固定する。`payload_digest` は、操作の意味に影響する引数を漏れなく対象としなければならない。

### **4.2 判定の結合**

Audit Decision を Action Request に適用するには、少なくとも以下が一致しなければならない。

* `action_type`  
* `subject_ref`  
* `target_ref`  
* `scope`  
* `canonical_history_ref`  
* 対象操作の payload またはその正規化済みダイジェスト  
* Policy Profile ID と Version  
* Decision の一意な識別子と評価結果

別操作、別対象、別スコープ、別 Payload に対する判定の流用は禁止する。

Decision の適用範囲が一部しか一致しない場合、暗黙に適用範囲を拡大してはならない。部分一致の扱いは、明示的な Policy Rule として定義され、検証されている場合に限る。

### **4.3 判定の意味**

`ALLOW` は、判定時点・指定スコープにおける Audit Decision の結果であり、単独では Authorization または Commit Admission を意味しない。

`ALLOW_WITH_EXCEPTION` は、例外の範囲・権限・期限・理由が別途検証されるまで Admission を許可しない。

`DENY`、`HOLD`、`NOT_EVALUATED` は、通常の Admission に対する許可根拠として使用してはならない。

## **5\. Authorization Evidence / Token Model**

### **5.1 認可証拠の必要属性**

認可証拠は、実装方式に応じて少なくとも以下を検証可能にする。

authorization\_evidence:  
  authorization\_id: REQUIRED  
  issuer\_identity: REQUIRED  
  subject\_identity: REQUIRED  
  action\_constraints: REQUIRED  
  resource\_constraints: REQUIRED  
  scope\_constraints: REQUIRED  
  policy\_profile\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_profile\_version: REQUIRED  
  issued\_at: REQUIRED  
  not\_before: REQUIRED  
  expires\_at: REQUIRED  
  revocation\_reference: REQUIRED  
  request\_binding\_digest: REQUIRED  
  evidence\_integrity: REQUIRED

この構造は論理モデルであり、最終 JSON Schema、署名方式、トークンエンコーディングを確定するものではない。

### **5.2 検証要件**

Admission 前に、Kernel は次を検証しなければならない。

1. 発行主体の信頼性。  
2. 証拠の完全性と真正性。  
3. 主体・操作・対象・スコープの一致。  
4. `not_before`、`expires_at` と現在時刻の適合性。  
5. 発行主体・委任元・権限の失効状態。  
6. 必要な Policy Profile の一致。  
7. Decision と Action Request の結合。  
8. リプレイ防止条件。  
9. 必要な依存 Proof の妥当性。  
10. 検証に必要な情報が欠落していないこと。

検証不能・不明・矛盾は成功として扱ってはならない。

### **5.3 最小権限**

認可は要求された Action・Target・Scope に必要な範囲に限定する。包括的な権限を、明示的な根拠なく個別操作へ転用してはならない。

認可証拠が正しく署名されていることは、その証拠に記載された権限が現在も有効であることを単独では保証しない。

## **6\. Freshness / Expiry / Revocation / Replay**

### **6.1 鮮度**

Admission は、Decision・Authorization・Policy Profile・対象 History の各依存関係が、その Admission 時点で適用可能であることを要求する。

FRESHNESS \=  
  DECISION\_VALID  
  AND AUTHORIZATION\_CURRENT  
  AND POLICY\_VERSION\_ACCEPTABLE  
  AND HISTORY\_PRECONDITION\_MATCHED  
  AND REVOCATION\_CHECK\_SATISFIED

これは論理的な必要条件を示す式であり、検証済み実装を意味しない。

### **6.2 有効期限と時計**

* 有効期限の境界条件は明示的に定義しなければならない。  
* 時刻源、時計ずれの許容範囲、単調時計の利用条件を定義しなければならない。  
* 時刻が信頼できない場合、期限検証を省略してはならない。  
* 有効期限の延長や時計ずれの許容は、黙示的に実施してはならない。

### **6.3 失効**

失効情報を確認できない場合の処理は Policy で明示する。失効確認が必須の権限については、失効状態が不明であることを有効と見なしてはならない。

失効の検出後は、影響を受ける未実行要求の Admission を拒否または保留する。すでに確定した Canonical History を、失効だけを理由に書き換えてはならない。

### **6.4 リプレイ防止**

同じ認可証拠や Decision の再提示を、別の操作または別の対象への新たな許可として扱ってはならない。

* `request_id` と `idempotency_key` の役割を区別する。  
* Admission の重複判定に必要な識別情報を保持する。  
* 同一キーに異なる Payload が結合された場合は拒否する。  
* 消費済みトークンの再使用規則を明示する。  
* 再試行と新規操作を、履歴上で識別可能にする。

## **7\. Deterministic Policy Execution**

### **7.1 入力の固定**

Policy Evaluation の入力には、必要に応じて以下を含める。

* 正規化済み Action Request  
* 対応する Audit Decision と Proof References  
* Authorization Evidence  
* Canonical History の対象位置または版  
* Policy Profile ID・Version  
* Trust / Revocation 状態  
* 明示的に許可された時刻入力  
* Validator / Evaluator のバージョン

### **7.2 決定論的評価**

同一の正規化入力、同一の Policy Profile、同一の明示的評価コンテキストに対して、Policy Evaluator は同一の結果を返さなければならない。

SAME\_INPUT  
\+ SAME\_POLICY\_VERSION  
\+ SAME\_EVALUATION\_CONTEXT  
\=\> SAME\_POLICY\_RESULT

未固定の環境変数、暗黙の既定値、非決定論的な LLM 出力を、確定的な Admission 根拠として使用してはならない。

外部情報が必要な場合は、情報の取得結果・取得時点・完全性・適用規則を入力として固定する。入力が固定できない場合は、評価結果を `INDETERMINATE` 相当として扱い、必要な Admission を行わない。

### **7.3 Policy Version**

Policy Profile の変更は、既存 Decision や Authorization に自動適用されない。旧版を使用できる条件、新版への再評価条件、失効時の扱いを明示しなければならない。

Policy Version が不明、未対応、または必要な意味論が解釈できない場合、古い版や弱いルールへの暗黙のダウングレードは禁止する。

## **8\. Commit Admission Preconditions**

Commit Admission は、少なくとも以下の条件がすべて成立した場合に限り許可される。

ADMISSION\_ALLOWED \=  
  REQUEST\_SCHEMA\_VALID  
  AND DECISION\_BOUND  
  AND DECISION\_ACCEPTABLE  
  AND AUTHORIZATION\_VALID  
  AND AUTHORIZATION\_CURRENT  
  AND POLICY\_EVALUATION\_PASS  
  AND PROOF\_REQUIREMENTS\_SATISFIED  
  AND HISTORY\_PRECONDITION\_MATCHED  
  AND TARGET\_STATE\_PRECONDITION\_MATCHED  
  AND IDEMPOTENCY\_CHECK\_PASS  
  AND TRUST\_REQUIREMENTS\_SATISFIED  
  AND ATOMIC\_BINDING\_AVAILABLE

ここで `PASS` は、必要な条件が検証済みであることを意味する。未評価、未対応、部分的な成功を `PASS` に変換してはならない。

### **8.1 Admission の記録**

Admission Record は少なくとも以下を記録する。

* Admission ID  
* Action Request ID と正規化済み要求のダイジェスト  
* Decision ID  
* Authorization ID または参照  
* Policy Profile ID・Version  
* History Precondition  
* 評価結果と失敗理由コード  
* Evaluator / Validator Version  
* 判定時刻と使用した時刻源  
* 関連する証拠参照

Admission Record は監査証拠であり、それ自体が Canonical History の Commit を意味するものではない。

### **8.2 拒否時の不変条件**

Admission が拒否・保留・未評価となった場合、対象操作による Canonical History の変更は禁止する。拒否監査記録の保存先が Canonical History と異なる場合、その記録の保存を Canonical Mutation と混同してはならない。

拒否記録を Canonical History に記録する設計を採用する場合、その記録操作自体に対する権限・Commit 規則を別途定義する。

## **9\. TOCTOU 防止 / Atomic Binding**

### **9.1 問題定義**

Policy Evaluation が成功した後、実際の Commit 前に次のいずれかが変化すると、Admission の根拠と実行対象が一致しなくなる。

* Canonical History の現在位置  
* 対象 Entity または Resource の状態  
* Authorization の失効状態  
* Policy Profile の適用状態  
* Action Payload  
* 対象 Scope  
* 必須 Proof の妥当性または有効性

### **9.2 必須制約**

1. Admission と Commit の間で、操作の意味を変える入力を差し替えてはならない。  
2. Admission 時に検証した要求と Commit 対象の要求は、正規化済み同一性条件を満たさなければならない。  
3. History Precondition が変化した場合、古い Admission を無条件に再利用してはならない。  
4. 認可の鮮度を維持できない場合、再評価または拒否を行う。  
5. 必須の検査と Commit の間に競合が発生した場合、競合検出と回復の規則を適用する。  
6. LLM や外部 Agent による再解釈を、Admission 済み要求の暗黙の変更として許容してはならない。

### **9.3 原子性の実現方式**

具体的な実装方式は未確定だが、少なくとも次のいずれかにより検査と実行の間の不整合を防止する必要がある。

* History Position に対する Compare-and-Swap。  
* Admission と Commit を結合するトランザクション。  
* 対象状態のバージョン検査を含む直列化可能な Commit 処理。  
* 同等の保証を持つ決定論的競合制御。

これらの方式は代替候補であり、すべてを同時に採用する要件ではない。採用方式の保証範囲と障害時の限界は明文化しなければならない。

## **10\. Retry / Idempotency / Unknown Commit Status**

### **10.1 状態の分離**

REQUEST\_RECEIVED  
  \-\> POLICY\_EVALUATED  
  \-\> ADMISSION\_GRANTED  
  \-\> COMMIT\_ATTEMPTED  
  \-\> COMMIT\_CONFIRMED  
   \| COMMIT\_REJECTED  
   \| COMMIT\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN

このライフサイクルは提案状態モデルである。`COMMIT_STATUS_UNKNOWN` は成功・失敗のいずれでもない。

### **10.2 冪等性**

同じ `idempotency_key` の再試行は、同じ論理操作に属することを検証しなければならない。

* キーと Payload の組合せが同一なら、既存操作の状態を照会してよい。  
* キーが同一で Payload が異なる場合は拒否する。  
* 既に Commit が確定している場合、再試行によって同一の論理操作を二重適用してはならない。  
* 失敗が確定していない操作を、失敗したものとして新規実行してはならない。

### **10.3 Commit 状態不明**

Commit 処理中のクラッシュ、応答喪失、タイムアウトなどで結果が不明になった場合：

1. 状態を `COMMIT_STATUS_UNKNOWN` として保持する。  
2. Canonical History または信頼できる Commit Receipt を用いて確定状態を照合する。  
3. 同一操作の確定が確認できた場合、既存結果に収束させる。  
4. 未確定であることを十分な根拠で確認できた場合のみ、規定された再試行経路へ進む。  
5. 照合不能の場合は保留し、重複 Commit を防止する。

`TIMEOUT != FAILURE`、`NO_RESPONSE != NO_COMMIT`、`RETRY != NEW_OPERATION` を維持する。

### **10.4 Admission の再利用**

Admission の再利用は、要求同一性、Policy Version、Authorization の有効性、History Precondition、リプレイ防止状態を再検証した場合に限る。Admission の過去の成功だけを理由に現在の実行を許可してはならない。

## **11\. Denial / Exception / Recovery**

### **11.1 拒否理由**

拒否は、可能な限り機械可読な理由コードに分類する。

REQUEST\_INVALID  
DECISION\_UNBOUND  
DECISION\_DENIED  
DECISION\_STALE  
AUTHORIZATION\_INVALID  
AUTHORIZATION\_EXPIRED  
AUTHORITY\_REVOKED  
POLICY\_DENIED  
POLICY\_INDETERMINATE  
PROOF\_REQUIREMENT\_UNSATISFIED  
HISTORY\_PRECONDITION\_MISMATCH  
TARGET\_STATE\_CONFLICT  
REPLAY\_DETECTED  
IDEMPOTENCY\_CONFLICT  
ATOMIC\_BINDING\_UNAVAILABLE  
TRUST\_STATE\_UNKNOWN  
COMMIT\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN  
INTERNAL\_VALIDATION\_FAILURE

これらは候補コードであり、正式な Closed Vocabulary としては未採用である。未知の理由コードを成功扱いしてはならない。

### **11.2 例外**

`ALLOW_WITH_EXCEPTION` は一般的な拒否回避手段ではない。例外を許可する場合は、少なくとも次を満たさなければならない。

* 例外を許可する主体が明示されている。  
* 例外の対象 Action・Resource・Scope が限定されている。  
* 理由、期限、根拠、監査参照が記録される。  
* 例外によって満たされない要件が明示される。  
* Canonical History の整合性や Commit Authority の排他的権限を破壊しない。  
* 未確認の Commit を確定済みに偽装しない。  
* 必須要件の免除が許可されていない場合、その要件を免除しない。

例外が許可されても、満たされていない要件を満たしたことにはならない。

### **11.3 復旧**

復旧は、失敗の種類に応じて以下から選択する。

* 入力修正後の新規要求。  
* Decision の再評価。  
* Authorization の再取得または再検証。  
* Policy Profile の明示的な再評価。  
* History Precondition の再取得。  
* Commit 状態の照合。  
* 権限を持つ主体による明示的な例外処理。  
* `HOLD` 状態の維持と人間による裁定。

復旧時に元の失敗記録を消去したり、拒否を成功として再分類したりしてはならない。

## **12\. Audit / Decision Provenance**

各 Admission の判断を、後から独立に検証できるようにする。

監査記録には、適用された入力、依存証拠、Decision、Authorization、Policy Profile、Evaluator Version、History Precondition、結果、理由コード、関連する Commit ID または状態不明参照を含める。

監査証跡は次を区別する。

* 要求の受領  
* Policy Evaluation の完了  
* Admission の許可・拒否・保留  
* Commit の試行  
* Commit の確定・拒否・状態不明  
* 失効検出  
* 例外の申請・許可・適用  
* 復旧・再評価

監査証跡の完全性と Canonical History の正当性は別々に検証する。監査記録が存在することだけを根拠に、操作が正当に Commit されたと結論づけてはならない。

## **13\. Failure Matrix**

| 障害・不整合 | Admission | Canonical Mutation | 必須対応 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Decision の対象不一致 | 拒否 | 禁止 | 再評価 |
| Authorization 期限切れ | 拒否 | 禁止 | 再認可 |
| 失効状態が不明（確認必須） | 拒否または保留 | 禁止 | 失効状態の照合 |
| Policy 評価が不確定 | 保留 | 禁止 | 入力・Policy の確定 |
| History Precondition 不一致 | 拒否 | 禁止 | 状態再取得・再評価 |
| 同一キー・異なる Payload | 拒否 | 禁止 | 衝突監査 |
| Admission と Commit の間の競合 | 中止または再評価 | 不正な変更は禁止 | 競合解決 |
| Commit 応答喪失 | 状態不明 | 推測による追加変更は禁止 | 状態照合 |
| 監査記録の保存失敗 | Policy に従い保留・拒否 | 要件に反する Commit を禁止 | 監査経路の復旧 |
| 未知の失敗コード | 拒否または保留 | 禁止 | 明示的な裁定 |

監査記録の保存失敗時に Commit を許可できるかは、監査の必須性、保存先、原子性保証によって決まる。要件が未定義のまま成功扱いしてはならない。

## **14\. Invariants — `STI-191-01..20`**

全件 `PROPOSED / NOT_VERIFIED`。

| ID | 不変条件 |
| ----- | ----- |
| STI-191-01 | Proof Validity と Audit Decision は別概念である。 |
| STI-191-02 | Audit Decision は Authorization を単独で構成しない。 |
| STI-191-03 | Authorization は Commit Admission を単独で構成しない。 |
| STI-191-04 | Commit Admission は Commit Finality を意味しない。 |
| STI-191-05 | Decision は Action・Target・Scope に結合される。 |
| STI-191-06 | Payload の意味的変更は既存 Decision の無条件再利用を無効にする。 |
| STI-191-07 | 期限切れ Authorization では Admission しない。 |
| STI-191-08 | 失効した権限を有効な認可として扱わない。 |
| STI-191-09 | 必須の失効確認が不明なら成功としない。 |
| STI-191-10 | Policy Evaluation は固定入力・固定版に対して決定論的である。 |
| STI-191-11 | 未対応 Policy Version への暗黙のダウングレードを禁止する。 |
| STI-191-12 | History Precondition の不一致は再検証を要求する。 |
| STI-191-13 | TOCTOU 防止の原子性または同等の競合制御を要求する。 |
| STI-191-14 | 拒否された要求は対象 Canonical History を変更しない。 |
| STI-191-15 | 同一 Idempotency Key と異なる Payload の組合せを拒否する。 |
| STI-191-16 | Retry は既存 Commit の重複適用を生じさせない。 |
| STI-191-17 | Commit 状態不明を成功・失敗へ推定変換しない。 |
| STI-191-18 | 例外は明示的な権限・対象・期限・理由を持つ。 |
| STI-191-19 | 監査証跡は Admission と Commit Finality を区別する。 |
| STI-191-20 | 不明・未評価・矛盾を `PASS` に変換しない。 |

## **15\. Test Candidates — `ST-191-01..20`**

全件 `NOT_EXECUTED`。以下は試験候補であり、実行結果ではない。

| ID | 試験対象 | 期待結果 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| ST-191-01 | 有効 Proof・拒否 Decision | Admission 拒否 |
| ST-191-02 | 別 Action の Decision 流用 | 拒否 |
| ST-191-03 | 別 Target の Authorization 流用 | 拒否 |
| ST-191-04 | Scope 拡大による再利用 | 拒否 |
| ST-191-05 | Admission 前の期限切れ | 拒否 |
| ST-191-06 | Admission 前の権限失効 | 拒否 |
| ST-191-07 | 必須の失効照会が失敗 | 拒否または保留 |
| ST-191-08 | 同一入力・同一 Policy Version の再評価 | 結果一致 |
| ST-191-09 | 未対応 Policy Version | 暗黙のダウングレードなし |
| ST-191-10 | History Position の競合 | Admission 無効化または再評価 |
| ST-191-11 | Admission 後の Payload 差し替え | Commit 拒否 |
| ST-191-12 | TOCTOU 競合の同時発生 | 不正な Commit を防止 |
| ST-191-13 | 拒否要求の実行 | 対象 Canonical History 不変 |
| ST-191-14 | 同一キー・同一 Payload の Retry | 二重適用なし |
| ST-191-15 | 同一キー・異なる Payload | 拒否 |
| ST-191-16 | Commit 成功後の応答喪失 | 状態照合により既存結果へ収束 |
| ST-191-17 | Commit 結果を照合できない | 状態不明を維持 |
| ST-191-18 | 必須要件を満たさない例外要求 | 拒否 |
| ST-191-19 | Admission Record と Commit Receipt の不一致 | 不整合検出 |
| ST-191-20 | 未知の判定値・理由コード | 成功扱いしない |

### **裁定**

本STEPの設計上の中心は、判定を得たこと、認可を得たこと、Commit Admission を通過したこと、Commit が確定したことを、それぞれ独立した検証可能な事実として扱うことである。

特に、Admission と Commit の間の TOCTOU、再試行による二重適用、Commit 結果不明時の推測確定は、単なるログ記録では解決しない。排他的な Commit Authority、永続的な操作識別、競合制御、状態照合の契約が必要となる。

このドラフトは設計提案として完成しているが、実装・試験・採用・Freeze は完了していない。

# **STEP-192 — Commit Admission Atomicity / Transaction Binding / Durable Commit Receipt / Recovery Reconciliation Contract**

STEP\_ID: 192  
VERSION: 0.1.0-draft  
STATUS: DRAFT\_COMPLETE  
NORMATIVE\_STATUS: PROPOSED  
ADOPTION\_STATUS: NOT\_ADOPTED  
IMPLEMENTATION\_STATUS: NOT\_VERIFIED  
TEST\_STATUS: NOT\_EXECUTED  
REPOSITORY\_INSPECTION: NOT\_PERFORMED  
TRANSACTION\_BOUNDARY\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
WAL\_ORDERING\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
COMMIT\_LINEARIZATION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
RECEIPT\_INTEGRITY\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
CRASH\_RECOVERY\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
IDEMPOTENCY\_RECONCILIATION\_VALIDATION: NOT\_EXECUTED  
CANONICAL\_MUTATION: NONE  
GLOBAL\_ACCEPTANCE: NOT\_ASSESSED  
FREEZE\_STATUS: NOT\_AUTHORIZED  
TIMEZONE: Asia/Tokyo

## **1\. 目的**

本契約は、STEP-191 の `Commit Admission` から Canonical History の確定までを対象に、トランザクション境界、永続化順序、Commit Receipt、障害復旧、重複実行防止を定義する。

中心となる要件は、操作の受理、Commit の試行、永続化、確定応答を同一の事実として扱わず、復旧後も Canonical History から操作の結果を一意に判定できることである。

対象：

1. Commit Intent と Action Request の固定。  
2. Admission と Commit の原子的結合。  
3. WAL と Canonical History の書込み順序。  
4. Commit の線形化点。  
5. Durable Commit Receipt の意味論。  
6. Crash Point ごとの復旧処理。  
7. Idempotency Record と History の整合。  
8. 部分書込み・応答喪失・再起動時の照合。  
9. Commit 状態不明の安全な維持。  
10. 監査証跡と Canonical History の整合性。

## **2\. 非目標**

* 既存 WAL 実装の実地検査。  
* 特定のデータベース、ファイルシステム、ストレージ方式の選定。  
* 実装の変更、テスト実行、性能評価。  
* Canonical History の既存レコードの修復・書換え。  
* Receipt の存在のみを根拠とする Commit 成功の推定。  
* 本ドラフト単独による採用・全体受入れ・Freeze。

## **3\. 規範原則**

ADMISSION\_GRANTED \!= COMMIT\_STARTED  
COMMIT\_STARTED \!= COMMIT\_DURABLE  
COMMIT\_DURABLE \!= RECEIPT\_DELIVERED  
RECEIPT\_DELIVERED \!= PROOF\_OF\_UNRELATED\_OPERATIONS

COMMIT\_CONFIRMED \=\> DURABLE\_CANONICAL\_EVIDENCE  
REJECTION \=\> NO\_TARGET\_CANONICAL\_MUTATION  
UNKNOWN\_COMMIT\_STATUS \!= COMMIT\_FAILURE  
UNKNOWN\_COMMIT\_STATUS \!= COMMIT\_SUCCESS

RETRY(SAME\_OPERATION) \=\> NO\_DUPLICATE\_CANONICAL\_EFFECT  
RECEIPT\_WITHOUT\_CANONICAL\_SUPPORT \!= COMMIT\_PROOF

### **3.1 権威境界**

* Kernel / Commit Authority：Commit の実行、永続化、確定状態の管理を担当する唯一の権威。  
* Canonical History / WAL：正規の操作結果を再構築・検証する根拠。  
* Commit Receipt：特定の Commit 結果に関する検証可能な証拠。  
* Idempotency Record：同一論理操作の重複適用を防ぐ補助記録。Canonical History と矛盾する場合に独立した真実として優先してはならない。  
* Projection / Chat / UI：Canonical History から導出される表示。Commit の確定権威ではない。  
* LLM / 外部 Agent：観測・提案のみ。Commit の確定権限を持たない。

## **4\. 用語**

* `Commit Intent`：Commit 対象操作と、その要求・Admission を固定した実行意図。  
* `Transaction Binding`：Commit Intent、Admission、操作識別子、対象 History の位置を検証可能な形で結び付けること。  
* `Linearization Point`：操作が論理的に確定したと見なされる単一の境界。  
* `Durable Commit`：採用する永続化契約の耐久性条件を満たし、再起動後も正規の操作結果として回復可能な Commit。  
* `Commit Receipt`：特定操作の確定結果と Canonical History 上の根拠を識別する証拠。  
* `Recovery Reconciliation`：再起動後、操作要求・WAL・Canonical History・Receipt・Idempotency Record を照合し、操作状態を確定または保留する処理。  
* `Crash Point`：操作ライフサイクルの特定位置でプロセスまたはシステムが停止する障害点。

## **5\. Commit Intent / Transaction Binding**

### **5.1 Commit Intent の論理モデル**

commit\_intent:  
  intent\_id: REQUIRED  
  operation\_id: REQUIRED  
  request\_id: REQUIRED  
  idempotency\_key: REQUIRED  
  action\_type: REQUIRED  
  subject\_ref: REQUIRED  
  target\_ref: REQUIRED  
  scope: REQUIRED  
  payload\_digest: REQUIRED  
  admission\_record\_ref: REQUIRED  
  authorization\_ref: REQUIRED  
  policy\_profile\_id: REQUIRED  
  policy\_profile\_version: REQUIRED  
  expected\_history\_position: REQUIRED  
  history\_identity: REQUIRED  
  created\_at: REQUIRED

これは提案中の論理モデルであり、正式な Schema、フィールド型、Digest 方式、識別子生成方式は未確定である。

### **5.2 不変の結合**

Commit 処理開始時に、少なくとも以下を一意に結合する。

1. Action Request の正規化済み内容。  
2. Admission Record。  
3. Authorization と Policy Profile の参照。  
4. Canonical History の識別子。  
5. 期待する History Position。  
6. Idempotency Key。  
7. Commit Intent / Operation ID。

Commit 対象の Payload、Action、Scope、Target、History Identity が変化した場合、既存 Admission をそのまま流用してはならない。

### **5.3 Operation Identity**

`operation_id` は、同一の論理操作を Commit 試行・復旧・再試行にわたって識別する。

`request_id` は要求の識別、`idempotency_key` は重複適用防止、`intent_id` は実行意図の識別に使用する。これらを同一フィールドとして扱う場合は、同等の識別保証を仕様として証明しなければならない。

同一 `operation_id` に異なる Payload Digest または異なる History Identity が結合された場合、競合として扱う。

## **6\. トランザクション境界と状態機械**

### **6.1 提案状態機械**

REQUEST\_VALIDATED  
  \-\> ADMISSION\_GRANTED  
  \-\> INTENT\_DURABLE  
  \-\> COMMIT\_PREPARED  
  \-\> CANONICAL\_APPEND\_ATTEMPTED  
  \-\> CANONICAL\_COMMIT\_DURABLE  
  \-\> RECEIPT\_DURABLE  
  \-\> COMMIT\_CONFIRMED

障害または競合が発生した場合の分岐：

ANY\_NONFINAL\_STATE  
  \-\> RECOVERY\_REQUIRED  
  \-\> RECONCILIATION\_IN\_PROGRESS  
  \-\> COMMIT\_CONFIRMED  
   \| COMMIT\_REJECTED  
   \| COMMIT\_STATUS\_UNKNOWN  
   \| RECOVERY\_BLOCKED

状態名と遷移は設計候補であり、既存実装で確認済みのものではない。

### **6.2 状態の意味**

* `INTENT_DURABLE`：Commit Intent が回復可能な形で保存されている。  
* `COMMIT_PREPARED`：必要な事前条件が確定し、Commit 試行へ進む状態。  
* `CANONICAL_APPEND_ATTEMPTED`：Canonical History への書込みを開始したが、確定は未証明。  
* `CANONICAL_COMMIT_DURABLE`：Canonical History の Commit が永続化契約を満たしたことが検証された状態。  
* `RECEIPT_DURABLE`：Commit Receipt が規定の耐久性条件を満たして保存された状態。  
* `COMMIT_CONFIRMED`：Canonical History の根拠により操作の確定が確認された状態。  
* `COMMIT_STATUS_UNKNOWN`：利用可能な証拠では成功・失敗を確定できない状態。  
* `RECOVERY_BLOCKED`：安全な自動復旧条件が成立せず、操作を保留している状態。

### **6.3 単一の確定境界**

システムは、Commit が論理的に確定する `Linearization Point` を定義しなければならない。

この境界は、単なる関数呼出し、メモリ上の更新、Receipt の生成、クライアントへの応答と同一とは限らない。

採用するストレージモデルに応じて、Linearization Point と耐久性条件の関係を明示する。永続化前の状態を確定済みとして外部に公開してはならない。

## **7\. WAL Ordering / Durable Commit**

### **7.1 永続化順序の要件**

実装が WAL を使用する場合、少なくとも次の関係を保証する必要がある。

1. Commit Intent と操作識別子を復旧可能にする。  
2. Commit の事前条件と History Precondition を検証する。  
3. Canonical History に適用する変更を、定義された形式で記録する。  
4. 規定の耐久性境界を満たしたことを確認する。  
5. Commit Receipt を生成・保存する。  
6. 確定結果を外部へ通知する。

ただし、上記の全段階を別々に永続化することが必須という意味ではない。Receipt と Canonical History を同一トランザクションで保存できる場合など、より強い原子性を持つ方式も許容候補とする。

規範要件： 外部への成功応答は、採用された永続化契約に従い、Canonical Commit の確定を証明できる時点より前に返してはならない。

### **7.2 WAL のレコード完全性**

WAL を使用する実装では、レコード境界、長さ、形式、チェックサムまたは同等の完全性検査、順序、トランザクション識別子を定義する。

部分書込みや破損レコードを完全な Commit として扱ってはならない。レコードの一部しか存在しない場合、回復処理は採用された WAL 契約に従ってその状態を識別しなければならない。

### **7.3 Flush と耐久性**

`write()` が成功したこと、ユーザー空間バッファからデータが送出されたこと、OS が書込みを受け付けたこと、ストレージ上で耐久化されたことを区別する。

耐久性保証は、実際のストレージ・ファイルシステム・同期 API・ハードウェア構成の保証に基づいて定義する。これらの保証が未確認の場合、`DURABLE` を宣言してはならない。

### **7.4 Commit の順序**

同一 Canonical History に対する複数 Commit は、History Position と確定順序の整合性を維持する。

* 先行 Commit の確定条件を満たす前に、後続 Commit を確定済みとして公開しない。  
* 競合する History Position を検出した場合、古い Admission に基づく書込みを禁止する。  
* WAL の再生順序と Canonical History の確定順序が一致することを検証可能にする。  
* 並列実行を採用する場合、直列実行と同等の規範的整合性を保証する。

## **8\. Commit Receipt Contract**

### **8.1 論理モデル**

commit\_receipt:  
  receipt\_id: REQUIRED  
  operation\_id: REQUIRED  
  intent\_id: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_history\_id: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_position: REQUIRED  
  committed\_payload\_digest: REQUIRED  
  commit\_result: REQUIRED  
  durable\_boundary\_id: REQUIRED  
  commit\_protocol\_version: REQUIRED  
  receipt\_created\_at: REQUIRED  
  integrity\_proof: REQUIRED

このモデルは提案であり、正式な Receipt Schema や署名形式は未確定である。

### **8.2 Receipt の意味**

Receipt は、特定の Operation ID、Canonical History、確定位置、Payload Digest に対応する Commit 結果を識別する。

Receipt の検証では少なくとも次を確認する。

1. Receipt の完全性と発行元。  
2. Operation ID と Commit Intent の一致。  
3. Canonical History Identity と Position の一致。  
4. Payload Digest と実際の確定内容の一致。  
5. 確定境界の証拠が採用プロトコルを満たすこと。  
6. 同一 Operation に対する Receipt の一意性規則。  
7. 既知の失効・矛盾・破損が存在しないこと。

Receipt の署名や完全性検査に成功しただけでは、記載された Commit が実際に Canonical History に永続化されていることの十分な証明にはならない。

### **8.3 Receipt の一意性**

同一論理操作に対する Receipt の重複生成・再送は、操作の二重 Commit を意味しない。

同じ Operation ID に複数 Receipt が存在する場合、同一の確定結果を指す再送なのか、異なる確定位置を指す競合なのかを区別する。後者は自動的に正常と判断せず、照合対象とする。

### **8.4 Receipt の欠落**

Canonical Commit が確定しているのに Receipt が欠落している場合、Commit の不存在を意味しない。Canonical History を根拠に結果を照合し、採用された規則に従って Receipt を再構成または再発行できるようにする。

Receipt が存在するのに Canonical History に対応する確定結果が存在しない場合、Receipt だけを根拠に Commit を捏造してはならない。プロトコル違反、破損、照合不可能な状態として扱う。

## **9\. Crash Recovery / Reconciliation**

### **9.1 復旧の権威**

復旧時の正規結果は Canonical History と、その永続化契約により定義される。

WAL、Commit Intent、Receipt、Idempotency Record は重要な証拠だが、各証拠の意味と優先関係を事前に定義する。矛盾した証拠を、任意の優先順位やタイムスタンプだけで解決してはならない。

### **9.2 照合アルゴリズムの要件**

復旧処理は、少なくとも以下を実施する。

1. WAL の完全性とレコード境界を検証する。  
2. 未完了の Commit Intent を列挙する。  
3. Operation ID ごとに Canonical History の確定状態を照合する。  
4. 対応する Receipt と Idempotency Record を照合する。  
5. 既存 Commit の重複適用を防止する。  
6. 証拠が十分な場合に限り、確定・未確定を分類する。  
7. 矛盾・破損・証拠不足を保留状態に分類する。  
8. 復旧判断と使用証拠を監査可能な形で残す。

### **9.3 復旧結果**

| 状態 | 判定条件 | 対応 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| `COMMIT_CONFIRMED` | Canonical Commit の確定証拠が十分 | 既存結果を返却・収束 |
| `COMMIT_REJECTED` | 未 Commit が確定し、拒否条件が証明済み | 拒否結果を記録 |
| `COMMIT_STATUS_UNKNOWN` | 成否を区別する証拠が不足 | 追加照合まで保留 |
| `RECOVERY_BLOCKED` | 証拠矛盾、破損、または安全な自動判断が不可能 | 自動 Commit を停止 |

`COMMIT_REJECTED` は、単に Canonical History 上で操作を発見できなかったことだけでは確定できない。検索範囲の完全性、WAL の回復可能性、永続化境界を含め、未 Commit を結論づけられる根拠が必要である。

### **9.4 復旧の冪等性**

同じ回復可能な状態に対して復旧処理を繰り返しても、Canonical History に二重の効果を生じさせてはならない。

復旧処理が Receipt の再発行、監査記録の追加、Idempotency Record の再構成を行う場合は、それぞれの副作用を個別に定義し、Canonical Commit の再実行と区別する。

## **10\. Crash Point Matrix**

| 障害点 | 復旧時の検証 | 禁止事項 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Intent 永続化前 | 要求と既存操作の存在確認 | Commit 済みと推定 |
| Intent 永続化後・Commit 前 | Intent、Admission、Authorization の照合 | 古い認可を無条件に再利用 |
| WAL 書込み途中 | レコード完全性とトランザクション状態の検証 | 部分レコードを Commit と認定 |
| WAL 書込み後・耐久化確認前 | 採用永続化契約に基づく照合 | 書込み成功だけで耐久性を断定 |
| Canonical Commit 後・Receipt 保存前 | History の確定状態を照合 | Receipt 欠落を未 Commit と断定 |
| Receipt 保存後・応答前 | History と Receipt の整合確認 | 同一操作を二重適用 |
| 応答送信途中 | Operation ID による結果照合 | タイムアウトを失敗と推定 |
| 再起動中の復旧処理 | 復旧処理の冪等性確認 | 未確定操作の並行再実行 |
| WAL と History の不整合 | 証拠・境界・回復規則の検証 | 任意の証拠を優先して上書き |
| Receipt と History の不整合 | Receipt の真正性と History の完全性を調査 | Receipt から Canonical History を捏造 |

## **11\. Idempotency / History Reconciliation**

### **11.1 Idempotency Record**

Idempotency Record は少なくとも以下を保持する。

idempotency\_record:  
  idempotency\_key: REQUIRED  
  operation\_id: REQUIRED  
  payload\_digest: REQUIRED  
  canonical\_history\_id: REQUIRED  
  operation\_status: REQUIRED  
  commit\_position: OPTIONAL  
  receipt\_ref: OPTIONAL  
  last\_reconciliation\_ref: OPTIONAL

`commit_position` と `receipt_ref` は、Commit が確定したときに限って有効な確定情報として扱う。

### **11.2 整合性規則**

* Idempotency Record の `COMMITTED` は、Canonical History の根拠と整合しなければならない。  
* Canonical History の確定済み操作に対し Idempotency Record が欠落している場合、二重 Commit を防ぐための再構成経路を定義する。  
* 同一キーに異なる Payload Digest が存在する場合、操作を自動統合してはならない。  
* Idempotency Record と Canonical History の状態が異なる場合、矛盾を記録して照合する。  
* Idempotency Record を削除・期限切れにする場合、重複 Commit 防止保証が失われないことを証明する。

### **11.3 保持期間**

保持期間は任意に短縮してはならない。少なくとも、操作の再送可能期間、障害復旧期間、監査要件、Operation ID の再利用規則との関係を定義する。

保持期間終了後も古い要求が再送され得る場合は、別の永続的な重複防止機構を必要とする。

## **12\. Audit Evidence / Canonical Consistency**

Commit の各段階について、監査証跡が何を証明するかを明示する。

| 証拠 | 証明できる対象 | 単独では証明できない対象 |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| Admission Record | Admission の判定と入力 | Commit の確定 |
| Commit Intent | 実行予定の操作 | 実際の書込み |
| WAL Record | WAL に記録された内容 | 規定の耐久性を満たしたこと |
| Canonical History Record | 正規履歴上の操作記録 | ストレージの耐久性契約が満たされたことの全条件 |
| Commit Receipt | 特定 Commit の結果に関する証拠 | History と矛盾する場合の Commit 成功 |
| Idempotency Record | 重複防止用の操作状態 | Canonical History と独立した確定真実 |
| Recovery Record | 復旧処理と判断根拠 | 判断自体の正当性 |

監査証跡は、各証拠の出所・バージョン・対象範囲・完全性を識別できなければならない。復旧処理によって証拠が追加・再構成された場合は、元の証拠と復旧生成物を区別する。

## **13\. Failure Impact / Containment**

| Failure Class | 影響 | 必須の封じ込め |
| ----- | ----- | ----- |
| `INTENT_CORRUPTION` | 操作同一性が不明 | Commit 停止 |
| `WAL_PARTIAL_WRITE` | Commit 状態が不明 | レコード検証と照合 |
| `DURABILITY_UNCONFIRMED` | 耐久性が未証明 | 確定応答を保留 |
| `HISTORY_WAL_CONFLICT` | 正規結果に矛盾 | 自動修復・上書きを禁止 |
| `RECEIPT_MISSING` | 応答証拠が欠落 | History による照合 |
| `RECEIPT_HISTORY_CONFLICT` | Receipt の正当性が不明 | Receipt を確定根拠として使用しない |
| `IDEMPOTENCY_DIVERGENCE` | 重複実行の可能性 | 新規適用を停止 |
| `DUPLICATE_OPERATION` | 二重 Commit の可能性 | Operation ID と History の照合 |
| `RECOVERY_NONDETERMINISM` | 復旧結果が不安定 | 自動復旧を停止 |
| `RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_GAP` | 成否の証明が不十分 | `COMMIT_STATUS_UNKNOWN` を維持 |

復旧処理は、証拠の不足を独自の推論で補完してはならない。証拠不足が解消されない場合、保留は正常な安全状態であり、処理を強制的に成功・失敗へ収束させる必要はない。
